Some historians argue that the award caused Britain to incur disproportionate losses in defending Malta, as British credibility would have suffered if Malta had surrendered, <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Singapore" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle of Singapore">as British forces in Singapore had done</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> A depiction of the George Cross now appears in the upper hoist corner of the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Malta" title="Flag of Malta">Flag of Malta</a> and on the country's <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Malta" title="Coat of arms of Malta">arms</a>. The collective award remained unique until April 1999, when the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Ulster_Constabulary" title="Royal Ulster Constabulary">Royal Ulster Constabulary</a> became the second recipient of a collective George Cross.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence_and_Republic">Independence and Republic</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/State_of_Malta" title="State of Malta">State of Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Maglio_gardens.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Maglio_gardens.jpg/220px-Maglio_gardens.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Maglio_gardens.jpg/330px-Maglio_gardens.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Maglio_gardens.jpg/440px-Maglio_gardens.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4486" data-file-height="2971" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Maglio_gardens.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Monument to the independence of Malta in <a href="/wiki/Floriana" title="Floriana">Floriana</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_(081).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg/220px-Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg/330px-Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg/440px-Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_%28081%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="521" data-file-height="310" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tratado_de_Lisboa_13_12_2007_(081).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Malta joined the European Union in 2004 and signed the <a href="/wiki/Lisbon_Treaty" class="mw-redirect" title="Lisbon Treaty">Lisbon Treaty</a> in 2007.</div></div></div>
<p>Malta achieved its independence as the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Malta" title="State of Malta">State of Malta</a> on 21 September 1964 (<a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(Malta)" title="Independence Day (Malta)">Independence Day</a>) after intense negotiations with the United Kingdom, led by Maltese Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/George_Bor%C4%A1_Olivier" class="mw-redirect" title="George Borġ Olivier">George Borġ Olivier</a>. Under its 1964 constitution, Malta initially retained Queen <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Elizabeth II of the United Kingdom">Elizabeth II</a> as <a href="/wiki/Queen_of_Malta" title="Queen of Malta">Queen of Malta</a> and thus <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>, with a <a href="/wiki/Governor-general" title="Governor-general">governor-general</a> exercising executive authority on her behalf. In 1971, the <a href="/wiki/Malta_Labour_Party" class="mw-redirect" title="Malta Labour Party">Malta Labour Party</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Dom_Mintoff" title="Dom Mintoff">Dom Mintoff</a> won the general elections, resulting in Malta declaring itself a republic on 13 December 1974 (<a href="/wiki/Republic_Day_(Malta)" title="Republic Day (Malta)">Republic Day</a>) within the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a>, with a <a href="/wiki/President_of_Malta" title="President of Malta">president</a> as <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>. A defence agreement was signed soon after independence, and after being re-negotiated in 1972, expired on 31 March 1979 (<a href="/wiki/Freedom_Day_(Malta)" title="Freedom Day (Malta)">Freedom Day</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Upon its expiry, the British base closed down and all lands formerly controlled by the British on the island were given up to the Maltese government.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In the aftermath of the departure of the remaining British troops from the island in 1979 the country intensified <a href="/wiki/Malta_and_the_Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Malta and the Non-Aligned Movement">its participation in the Non-Aligned Movement</a>.
</p><p>Malta adopted a policy of <a href="/wiki/Neutral_country" title="Neutral country">neutrality</a> in 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> In 1989, Malta was the venue of a <a href="/wiki/Malta_Summit" title="Malta Summit">summit</a> between US President <a href="/wiki/George_H._W._Bush" title="George H. W. Bush">George H. W. Bush</a> and Soviet leader <a href="/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev" title="Mikhail Gorbachev">Mikhail Gorbachev</a>, their first face-to-face encounter, which signalled the end of the <a href="/wiki/Cold_War" title="Cold War">Cold War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 16 July 1990, Malta, through its foreign minister, <a href="/wiki/Guido_de_Marco" title="Guido de Marco">Guido de Marco</a>, applied to join the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> After tough negotiations, a referendum was held on 8 March 2003, which resulted in a favourable vote.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> General Elections held on 12 April 2003, gave a clear mandate to the Prime Minister, <a href="/wiki/Eddie_Fenech_Adami" title="Eddie Fenech Adami">Eddie Fenech Adami</a>, to sign the treaty of accession to the European Union on 16 April 2003 in <a href="/wiki/Athens" title="Athens">Athens</a>, Greece.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Malta joined the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> on 1 May 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Following the European Council of 21–22 June 2007, Malta joined the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">eurozone</a> on 1 January 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Malta" title="Politics of Malta">Politics of Malta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Malta" title="Government of Malta">Government of Malta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Malta" title="Law of Malta">Law of Malta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Malta" title="Foreign relations of Malta">Foreign relations of Malta</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <h3><span class="mw-headline" id="National">National</span></h3>
<p>Malta is a <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a><sup id="cite_ref-constitution_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> whose <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary system</a> and <a href="/wiki/Public_administration" title="Public administration">public administration</a> are closely modelled on the <a href="/wiki/Westminster_system" title="Westminster system">Westminster system</a>.
</p><p>Malta had the second-highest <a href="/wiki/Voter_turnout" title="Voter turnout">voter turnout</a> in the world (and the highest for nations without <a href="/wiki/Mandatory_voting" class="mw-redirect" title="Mandatory voting">mandatory voting</a>), based on election turnout in national <a href="/wiki/Lower_house" title="Lower house">lower house</a> elections from 1960 to 1995.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> Parliament is made up of the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Malta" title="President of Malta">President of Malta</a> and the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_of_Malta" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Representatives of Malta">House of Representatives</a> (<a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese</a>: <i lang="mt">Kamra tad-Deputati</i>). The President of Malta, a largely ceremonial position, is appointed for a five-year term by a resolution of the House of Representatives carried by a simple majority.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="2340" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta%E2%80%99s_Parliament_building_in_Valletta_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_House_(Malta)" title="Parliament House (Malta)">Parliament House</a> in Valletta</div></div></div>
<p>The House of Representatives has 65 members, elected for a five-year term in 13 five-seat electoral divisions, called <i>distretti elettorali</i>, with constitutional amendments that allow for mechanisms to establish strict proportionality amongst seats and votes of political parliamentary groups.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Members of the House of Representatives are elected by direct universal suffrage through <a href="/wiki/Single_transferable_vote" title="Single transferable vote">single transferable vote</a> every five years, unless the House is dissolved earlier by the president either on the advice of the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Malta" title="Prime Minister of Malta">prime minister</a> or through the adoption of a motion of no confidence carried within the House of Representatives and not overturned within three days. In either of these cases, the president may alternatively choose to invite another Member of Parliament who invariably should command the majority of the House of Representatives to form an alternative government for the remainder of the legislature.
</p><p>The House of Representatives is nominally made up of 65 members of parliament whereby 5 members of parliament are elected from each of the thirteen electoral districts. However, where a party wins an absolute majority of votes but does not have a majority of seats, that party is given additional seats to ensure a parliamentary majority. The 80th article of the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Malta" title="Constitution of Malta">Constitution of Malta</a> provides that the president appoint as prime minister "... the member of the House of Representatives who, in his judgment, is best able to command the support of a majority of the members of that House".<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_34-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Auberge_de_Castille,_Valletta_001.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg/220px-Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg/330px-Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg/440px-Auberge_de_Castille%2C_Valletta_001.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3949" data-file-height="2470" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Auberge_de_Castille,_Valletta_001.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Auberge_de_Castille" title="Auberge de Castille">Auberge de Castille</a> houses the Office of the Prime Minister of Malta</div></div></div>
<p>Maltese politics is a <a href="/wiki/Two-party_system" title="Two-party system">two-party system</a> dominated by the <a href="/wiki/Labour_Party_(Malta)" title="Labour Party (Malta)">Labour Party</a> (<a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese</a>: <i lang="mt">Partit Laburista</i>), a centre-left <a href="/wiki/Social_democracy" title="Social democracy">social democratic</a> party, and the <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_Party_(Malta)" title="Nationalist Party (Malta)">Nationalist Party</a> (<a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese</a>: <i lang="mt">Partit Nazzjonalista</i>), a centre-right <a href="/wiki/Christian_democracy" title="Christian democracy">Christian democratic</a> party. The Labour Party has been the governing party since 2013 and is currently led by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Robert_Abela" title="Robert Abela">Robert Abela</a>, who has been in office since 13 January 2020. The Nationalist Party, with <a href="/wiki/Bernard_Grech" title="Bernard Grech">Bernard Grech</a> as its leader, is currently in opposition. There are a number of small political parties in Malta which have no parliamentary representation.
</p><p>Until the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>, Maltese politics was dominated by the <a href="/wiki/Language_Question_(Malta)" title="Language Question (Malta)">Language Question</a> fought out by <a href="/wiki/Italophone" class="mw-redirect" title="Italophone">Italophone</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anglophone" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglophone">Anglophone</a> parties.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Post-war politics dealt with constitutional questions on the relations with Britain (first with <a href="/wiki/Maltese_integration_into_the_United_Kingdom_referendum,_1956" class="mw-redirect" title="Maltese integration into the United Kingdom referendum, 1956">integration</a> then <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(Malta)" title="Independence Day (Malta)">independence</a>) and, eventually, <a href="/wiki/History_of_Malta#The_accession_process_to_the_European_Union_(1987–2004)" title="History of Malta">relations with the European Union</a>.
</p><p>Since Malta is a republic, the head of state in Malta is the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Malta" title="President of Malta">President of the Republic</a>. The current President of the Republic is <a href="/wiki/George_Vella" title="George Vella">George Vella</a>, who was appointed in 2019 after being nominated both by the <a href="/wiki/Labour_Party_(Malta)" title="Labour Party (Malta)">Labour Party</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_Party_(Malta)" title="Nationalist Party (Malta)">Nationalist Party</a> as opposition.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> He is the tenth president to be appointed.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Local_councils_of_Malta" title="Local councils of Malta">Local councils of Malta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Malta" title="Districts of Malta">Districts of Malta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_Malta" title="Regions of Malta">Regions of Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_administrative_division.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Malta_-_administrative_division.svg/220px-Malta_-_administrative_division.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Malta_-_administrative_division.svg/330px-Malta_-_administrative_division.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Malta_-_administrative_division.svg/440px-Malta_-_administrative_division.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_administrative_division.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Local_councils_of_Malta" title="Local councils of Malta">Administrative divisions</a> of Malta</div></div></div>
<p>Malta has had a system of local government since 1993,<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> based on the <a href="/wiki/European_Charter_of_Local_Self-Government" title="European Charter of Local Self-Government">European Charter of Local Self-Government</a>. The country is divided into <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_Malta" title="Regions of Malta">five regions</a> (one of them being Gozo), with each region having its own Regional Committee, serving as the intermediate level between local government and national government.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> The regions are divided into <a href="/wiki/Local_councils_of_Malta" title="Local councils of Malta">local councils</a>, of which there are currently 68 (54 in Malta and 14 in <a href="/wiki/Gozo" title="Gozo">Gozo</a>). The <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Malta" title="Districts of Malta">six districts</a> (five on Malta and the sixth being Gozo) serve primarily statistical purposes.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Each council is made up of a number of councillors (from 5 to 13, depending on and relative to the population they represent). A mayor and a <a href="/wiki/Deputy_mayor" title="Deputy mayor">deputy mayor</a> are elected by and from the councillors. The executive secretary, who is appointed by the council, is the executive, administrative and financial head of the council. Councillors are elected every four years through the <a href="/wiki/Single_transferable_vote" title="Single transferable vote">single transferable vote</a>. People who are eligible to vote in the election of the Maltese <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_of_Malta" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Representatives of Malta">House of Representatives</a> as well as a resident <a href="/wiki/Citizenship_of_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Citizenship of the European Union">citizens of the EU</a> are eligible to vote. Due to system reforms, no elections were held before 2012. Since then, elections have been held every two years for an alternating half of the councils.
</p><p>Local councils are responsible for the general upkeep and embellishment of the locality (including repairs to non-arterial roads), allocation of local wardens, and refuse collection; they also carry out general administrative duties for the central government such as the collection of government rents and funds and answer government-related public inquiries. Additionally, a number of individual towns and villages in the Republic of Malta have <a href="/wiki/List_of_twin_towns_and_sister_cities_in_Malta" title="List of twin towns and sister cities in Malta">sister cities</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Malta" title="Armed Forces of Malta">Armed Forces of Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg/220px-US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg/330px-US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg/440px-US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4145" data-file-height="2770" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:US_Navy_111205-N-FV216-139_The_Armed_Forces_of_Malta_counter_piracy_vessel_protection_detachment_demonstrates_aerial_boarding_procedures_during_Eur.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Protector-class_coastal_patrol_boat" title="Protector-class coastal patrol boat"><i>Protector</i>-class patrol boats</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Maritime_Squadron_of_the_Armed_Forces_of_Malta" title="Maritime Squadron of the Armed Forces of Malta">Maritime Squadron of the AFM</a></div></div></div>
<p>The objectives of the <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Malta" title="Armed Forces of Malta">Armed Forces of Malta</a> (AFM) are to maintain a military organisation with the primary aim of defending the islands' integrity according to the defence roles as set by the government in an efficient and cost-effective manner. This is achieved by emphasising the maintenance of Malta's territorial waters and airspace integrity.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The AFM also engages in combating terrorism, fighting against illicit drug trafficking, conducting anti-illegal immigrant operations and patrols, and anti-illegal fishing operations, operating <a href="/wiki/Search_and_rescue" title="Search and rescue">search and rescue</a> (SAR) services, and physical or electronic security and surveillance of sensitive locations. Malta's search-and-rescue area extends from east of <a href="/wiki/Tunisia" title="Tunisia">Tunisia</a> to west of <a href="/wiki/Crete" title="Crete">Crete</a>, covering an area of around 250,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (97,000&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a military organisation, the AFM provides backup support to the <a href="/wiki/Malta_Police_Force" title="Malta Police Force">Malta Police Force</a> (MPF) and other government departments/agencies in situations as required in an organised, disciplined manner in the event of national emergencies (such as natural disasters) or internal security and bomb disposal.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2020, Malta signed and ratified the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Malta" title="Geography of Malta">Geography of Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:General_map_of_Malta.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/General_map_of_Malta.svg/220px-General_map_of_Malta.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="190" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/General_map_of_Malta.svg/330px-General_map_of_Malta.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/General_map_of_Malta.svg/440px-General_map_of_Malta.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="502" data-file-height="433" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:General_map_of_Malta.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Topographic map of Malta</div></div></div>
<p>Malta is an <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a> in the central <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean" class="mw-redirect" title="Mediterranean">Mediterranean</a> (in its <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean" title="Eastern Mediterranean">eastern basin</a>), some 80&#160;km (50&#160;mi) from southern <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> across the <a href="/wiki/Malta_Channel" title="Malta Channel">Malta Channel</a>. Only the three largest islands—<a href="/wiki/Malta_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Malta Island">Malta</a> (Malta), <a href="/wiki/Gozo" title="Gozo">Gozo</a> (Għawdex), and <a href="/wiki/Comino" title="Comino">Comino</a> (Kemmuna)—are inhabited. The islands of the archipelago lie on the Malta plateau, a shallow shelf formed from the high points of a <a href="/wiki/Land_bridge" title="Land bridge">land bridge</a> between Sicily and North Africa that became isolated as sea levels rose after the <a href="/wiki/Last_Glacial_Period" title="Last Glacial Period">last ice age</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago is located on the <a href="/wiki/African_plate" class="mw-redirect" title="African plate">African tectonic plate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
Malta was considered an island of North Africa for centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Numerous bays along the indented coastline of the islands provide good harbours. The landscape consists of low hills with terraced fields. The highest point in Malta is <a href="/wiki/Ta%27_Dmejrek" title="Ta&#39; Dmejrek">Ta' Dmejrek</a>, at 253&#160;m (830&#160;ft), near <a href="/wiki/Dingli" title="Dingli">Dingli</a>. Although there are some small rivers at times of high rainfall, there are no permanent rivers or lakes on Malta. However, some watercourses have fresh water running all year round at <a href="/wiki/Ba%C4%A7rija" title="Baħrija">Baħrija</a> near <a href="/wiki/Ras_ir-Ra%C4%A7eb" title="Ras ir-Raħeb">Ras ir-Raħeb</a>, at l-Imtaħleb and San Martin, and at Lunzjata Valley in Gozo.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Phytogeography" title="Phytogeography">Phytogeographically</a>, Malta belongs to the Liguro-Tyrrhenian province of the <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Mediterranean Region">Mediterranean Region</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom" title="Boreal Kingdom">Boreal Kingdom</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature" title="World Wide Fund for Nature">WWF</a>, the territory of Malta belongs to the terrestrial <a href="/wiki/Ecoregion" title="Ecoregion">ecoregion</a> of <a href="/wiki/Tyrrhenian-Adriatic_sclerophyllous_and_mixed_forests" class="mw-redirect" title="Tyrrhenian-Adriatic sclerophyllous and mixed forests">Tyrrhenian-Adriatic sclerophyllous and mixed forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_Mgarr_(Fort_Bingemma_)_01_ies.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg/220px-Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg/330px-Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg/440px-Malta_-_Mgarr_%28Fort_Bingemma_%29_01_ies.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5616" data-file-height="3744" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_Mgarr_(Fort_Bingemma_)_01_ies.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Maltese landscape, <a href="/wiki/M%C4%A1arr" title="Mġarr">Mġarr</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The following uninhabited minor islands are part of the archipelago:
</p>

</div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Malta" title="Climate of Malta">Climate of Malta</a></div>
<p>Malta has a <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_climate" title="Mediterranean climate">Mediterranean climate</a> (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a> <i>Csa</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-cia_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> with mild winters and hot summers, hotter in the inland areas. Rain occurs mainly in autumn and winter, with summer being generally dry.
</p><p>The average yearly <a href="/wiki/Temperature" title="Temperature">temperature</a> is around 23&#160;°C (73&#160;°F) during the day and 15.5&#160;°C (59.9&#160;°F) at night. In the coldest month&#160;– January&#160;– the typical maximum temperature ranges from 12 to 18&#160;°C (54 to 64&#160;°F) during the day and minimum 6 to 12&#160;°C (43 to 54&#160;°F) at night. In the warmest month&#160;– August&#160;– the typical maximum temperature ranges from 28 to 34&#160;°C (82 to 93&#160;°F) during the day and minimum 20 to 24&#160;°C (68 to 75&#160;°F) at night. Amongst all capitals in the continent of Europe, Valletta&#160;– the capital of Malta has the warmest winters, with average temperatures of around 15 to 16&#160;°C (59 to 61&#160;°F) during the day and 9 to 10&#160;°C (48 to 50&#160;°F) at night in the period January–February. In March and December average temperatures are around 17&#160;°C (63&#160;°F) during the day and 11&#160;°C (52&#160;°F) at night.<sup id="cite_ref-Met_Office_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Met_Office-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Large fluctuations in temperature are rare. Snow is very rare on the island, although various snowfalls have been recorded in the last century, the last one reported in various locations across Malta in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The average annual sea temperature is 20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F), from 15–16&#160;°C (59–61&#160;°F) in February to 26&#160;°C (79&#160;°F) in August. In the 6 months&#160;– from June to November&#160;– the average sea temperature exceeds 20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-weather2travel_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-weather2travel-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-maltaweather_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maltaweather-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The annual average <a href="/wiki/Relative_humidity" class="mw-redirect" title="Relative humidity">relative humidity</a> is high, averaging 75%, ranging from 65% in July (morning: 78% evening: 53%) to 80% in December (morning: 83% evening: 73%).<sup id="cite_ref-weatherbase.com_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-weatherbase.com-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Sunshine_duration" title="Sunshine duration">Sunshine duration</a> hours total around 3,000 per year, from an average 5.2 hours of sunshine duration per day in December to an average above 12 hours in July.<sup id="cite_ref-maltaweather_167-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maltaweather-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-noaa_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-noaa-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> This is about double that of cities in the northern half of <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:No_original_research" title="Wikipedia:No original research"><span title="The material near this tag possibly contains original research. (March 2020)">original research?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> for comparison: London&#160;– 1,461;<sup id="cite_ref-London_climate_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-London_climate-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> however, in winter it has up to four times more sunshine; for comparison: in December, <a href="/wiki/London" title="London">London</a> has 37 hours of sunshine<sup id="cite_ref-London_climate_171-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-London_climate-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> whereas Malta has above 160.
</p>
