<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Urbanisation">Urbanisation</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Valletta,_Malta.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG/220px-Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG/330px-Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG/440px-Valletta%2C_Malta.JPG 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="437" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Valletta,_Malta.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The main urban area of Malta. Valletta is the central peninsula.</div></div></div>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a>, Malta is composed of two <a href="/wiki/Larger_urban_zones" class="mw-redirect" title="Larger urban zones">larger urban zones</a> nominally referred to as "Valletta" (the main island of Malta) and "Gozo". The main urban area covers the entire main island, with a population of around 400,000.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The core of the urban area, the <i>greater city</i> of Valletta, has a population of 205,768.<sup id="cite_ref-Eurostat-city_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eurostat-city-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> According to the data from 2020 by <a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a>, the Functional Urban Area and metropolitan region covered the whole island and has a population of 480 134.<sup id="cite_ref-Eurostat_FUA_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eurostat_FUA-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Eurostat-metro_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eurostat-metro-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> According to the United Nations, about 95 percent of the area of Malta is urban and the number grows every year.<sup id="cite_ref-WUP_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WUP-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Also, according to the results of ESPON and EU Commission studies, "<i>the whole territory of Malta constitutes a single urban region</i>".<sup id="cite_ref-ESPON-EUC_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ESPON-EUC-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Occasionally in books,<sup id="cite_ref-GMB_Publishing_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GMB_Publishing-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> government publications and documents,<sup id="cite_ref-creativemalta_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-creativemalta-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-doi_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-doi-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> and in some international institutions,<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> Malta is referred to as a <a href="/wiki/City-state" title="City-state">city-state</a>. Sometimes Malta is listed in rankings concerning cities<sup id="cite_ref-GFC_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GFC-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> or metropolitan areas.<sup id="cite_ref-inta-aivn.org_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-inta-aivn.org-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Also, the Maltese coat-of-arms bears a <a href="/wiki/Mural_crown" title="Mural crown">mural crown</a> described as "representing the fortifications of Malta and denoting a City State".<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> Malta, with area of 316&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (122&#160;sq&#160;mi) and population of over 0.5&#160;million, is one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_population_density" class="mw-redirect" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by population density">most densely populated</a> countries worldwide.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">WHO</a> reassigned the Islands and Small States Institute in Malta on 29 April 2022 as a collaborating center that included heavy work on topics like the development of policy recommendations on building health-system resilience in small states, the interrelationship between <a href="/wiki/Tourism" title="Tourism">tourism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Health_system" title="Health system">health systems</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sustainability" title="Sustainability">sustainability</a>, with a focus on islands and small countries, through a planetary health and equity approach and the development of a toolkit on health information, digital health and evidence generation in small states.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png/220px-%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png/330px-%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png/440px-%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png 2x" data-file-width="950" data-file-height="665" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%9F%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BE%D1%86%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%83%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%8F_%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8C%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%B9%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%8F.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>National plant: <a href="/wiki/Cheirolophus_crassifolius" title="Cheirolophus crassifolius">Maltese centaury</a> (<span title="Maltese-language text"><i lang="mt">Widnet il&#x2011;Baħar</i></span>, since 1971)</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Flora_of_Malta" title="Flora of Malta">Flora of Malta</a></div>
<p>The Maltese islands are home to a wide diversity of indigenous, sub-endemic and endemic plants.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> They feature many traits typical of a Mediterranean climate, such as drought resistance. The most common indigenous trees on the islands are olive (<a href="/wiki/Olive" title="Olive"><i>Olea europaea</i></a>), carob (<i><a href="/wiki/Ceratonia_siliqua" class="mw-redirect" title="Ceratonia siliqua">Ceratonia siliqua</a></i>), fig (<a href="/wiki/Common_fig" class="mw-redirect" title="Common fig"><i>ficus carica</i></a>), holm oak (<i><a href="/wiki/Quercus_ilex" title="Quercus ilex">Quericus ilex</a></i>) and Aleppo pine (<i><a href="/wiki/Pinus_halepensis" title="Pinus halepensis">Pinus halepensis</a></i>), while the most common non-native trees are <a href="/wiki/Eucalyptus" title="Eucalyptus">eucalyptus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Acacia" title="Acacia">acacia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Opuntia_ficus-indica" title="Opuntia ficus-indica">opuntia</a>. Endemic plants include the national flower <i>widnet il-baħar</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Cheirolophus_crassifolius" title="Cheirolophus crassifolius">Cheirolophus crassifolius</a>), sempreviva ta' Malta (<a href="/wiki/Helichrysum_melitense" title="Helichrysum melitense">Helichrysum melitense</a>),</i> <i>żigland t' Għawdex (<a href="/wiki/Hyoseris_frutescens" title="Hyoseris frutescens">Hyoseris frutescens</a></i>) and <i>ġiżi ta' Malta</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Matthiola_incana" title="Matthiola incana">Matthiola incana subsp. melitensis</a></i>) while sub-endemics include <i>kromb il-baħar</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Jacobaea_maritima" title="Jacobaea maritima">Jacobaea maritima subsp. sicula</a></i>) and <i>xkattapietra</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Micromeria_microphylla" title="Micromeria microphylla">Micromeria microphylla</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> The flora and biodiversity of Malta is severely endangered by habitat loss, invasive species and human intervention.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Malta" title="Economy of Malta">Economy of Malta</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">December 2019</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="General">General</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Malta_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Malta exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Malta is classified as an <a href="/wiki/Advanced_economy" class="mw-redirect" title="Advanced economy">advanced economy</a> together with 32 other countries according to the International Monetary Fund (IMF).<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Until 1800, Malta depended on cotton, tobacco and its shipyards for exports. Once under British control, they came to depend on <a href="/wiki/Malta_Dockyard" title="Malta Dockyard">Malta Dockyard</a> for support of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a>, especially during the <a href="/wiki/Crimean_War" title="Crimean War">Crimean War</a> of 1854. The military base benefited craftsmen and all those who served the military.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1869, the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Suez_Canal" title="Suez Canal">Suez Canal</a> gave Malta's economy a great boost, as there was a massive increase in the shipping which entered the port. Ships stopping at Malta's docks for refuelling helped the <a href="/wiki/Entrep%C3%B4t" title="Entrepôt">Entrepôt</a> trade, which brought additional benefits to the island. However, towards the end of the 19th century, the economy began declining, and by the 1940s Malta's economy was in serious crisis. One factor was the longer range of newer <a href="/wiki/Merchant_ship" title="Merchant ship">merchant ships</a> that required fewer refuelling stops.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG/220px-Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG/330px-Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG/440px-Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mediterraneo_dolphin_show_1.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The dolphin show at Mediterraneo Marine Park. Tourism generates a significant part of the GDP of Malta.</div></div></div>
<p>Currently,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (December 2019)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Malta's major resources are <a href="/wiki/Limestone" title="Limestone">limestone</a>, a favourable geographic location and a productive labour force. Malta produces only about 20 percent of its food needs, has limited fresh water supplies because of the drought in the summer, and has no domestic energy sources, aside from the potential for solar energy from its plentiful sunlight. The economy is dependent on foreign trade (serving as a freight trans-shipment point), manufacturing (especially electronics and textiles), and tourism.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Access to <a href="/wiki/Biocapacity" title="Biocapacity">biocapacity</a> in Malta is below the world average. In 2016, Malta had 0.6 global hectares of biocapacity per person within its territory, contrasted with a global average of 1.6 hectares per person.<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, residents of Malta exhibited an <a href="/wiki/Ecological_footprint" title="Ecological footprint">ecological footprint</a> of consumption of 5.8 global hectares of biocapacity per person, resulting in a sizable biocapacity deficit.<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Films_shot_in_Malta" class="mw-redirect" title="Films shot in Malta">Film production</a> has contributed to the Maltese economy.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> The film <i>Sons of the Sea</i> was the first shot in Malta, in 1925;<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> by 2016, over 100 feature films had been entirely or partially filmed in the country since. Malta has served as a "double" for a wide variety of locations and historic periods including <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greece" title="Ancient Greece">Ancient Greece</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Rome" title="Ancient Rome">Ancient</a> and modern <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, Iraq, the <a href="/wiki/Middle_East" title="Middle East">Middle East</a> and many more.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> The Maltese government introduced financial incentives for filmmakers in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> The current financial incentives to foreign productions as of 2015 stand at 25 per cent with an additional 2 per cent if Malta stands in as Malta; meaning a production can get up to 27 per cent back on their eligible spending incurred in Malta.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/220px-BlueEurozone.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/330px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/BlueEurozone.svg/440px-BlueEurozone.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="645" data-file-height="690" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BlueEurozone.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Malta is part of a <a href="/wiki/Monetary_union" class="mw-redirect" title="Monetary union">monetary union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">eurozone</a> (dark blue)</div></div></div>
<p>In preparation for Malta's membership in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, which it joined on 1 May 2004, it <a href="/wiki/Privatization" title="Privatization">privatised</a> some state-controlled firms and liberalised markets. For example, the government announced on 8 January 2007 that it was selling its 40 per cent stake in <a href="/wiki/MaltaPost" title="MaltaPost">MaltaPost</a>, to complete a privatisation process which had been ongoing for the previous five years.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> From 2000 to 2010, Malta privatised <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications" title="Telecommunications">telecommunications</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> postal services, <a href="/wiki/Shipyard" title="Shipyard">shipyards</a><sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Malta_International_Airport" title="Malta International Airport">Malta International Airport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Malta has a financial regulator, the <a href="/wiki/Malta_Financial_Services_Authority" title="Malta Financial Services Authority">Malta Financial Services Authority</a> (MFSA), with a strong business development mindset, and the country has been successful in attracting gaming businesses, aircraft and ship registration, credit-card issuing banking licences and also fund administration. Service providers to these industries, including fiduciary and trustee business, are a core part of the growth strategy of the island. Malta has made strong headway in implementing EU Financial Services Directives including UCITs IV and Alternative Investment Fund Managers (AIFMs). As a base for alternative asset managers who must comply with new directives, Malta has attracted a number of key players including IDS, Iconic Funds, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Apex_Fund_Services&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Apex Fund Services (page does not exist)">Apex Fund Services</a> and TMF/Customs House.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Malta and <a href="/wiki/Tunisia" title="Tunisia">Tunisia</a> in 2006 discussed the commercial exploitation of the continental shelf between their countries, particularly for petroleum exploration.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> These discussions are also undergoing between Malta and <a href="/wiki/Libya" title="Libya">Libya</a> for similar arrangements.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2015, Malta did not have a property tax. Its property market, especially around the harbour area, was booming, with the prices of apartments in some towns like St Julian's, Sliema and Gzira skyrocketing.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to <a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a> data, Maltese GDP per capita stood at <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_European_Union#Richest_and_poorest_NUTS_regions_(GDP_PPP_2010)" title="Economy of the European Union">88 per cent of the EU average</a> in 2015 with €21,000.<sup id="cite_ref-Eurostat_2012_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eurostat_2012-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The National Development and Social Fund from the Individual Investor Programme, a <a href="/wiki/Immigrant_investor_programs" title="Immigrant investor programs">citizenship by investment programme</a> also known as the "citizenship scheme", has become a significant income source for the government of Malta, adding 432,000,000 euro to the budget in 2018. This 'scheme' has very low due diligence and many doubtful Russian, Middle-eastern and Chinese have obtained a Maltese passport, which is also a European Union passport. In July 2020, the Labour government admitted this and has opted to stop it as of September 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Banking_and_finance">Banking and finance</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_(Triq_it-Torri_(Sliema))_03_ies.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg/220px-Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg/330px-Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg/440px-Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_%28Triq_it-Torri_%28Sliema%29%29_03_ies.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5616" data-file-height="3744" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_-_St._Julian%27s_(Triq_it-Torri_(Sliema))_03_ies.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Portomaso_Business_Tower" class="mw-redirect" title="Portomaso Business Tower">Portomaso Business Tower</a>, the tallest building in Malta</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_banks_in_Malta" title="List of banks in Malta">List of banks in Malta</a></div>
<p>The two largest commercial banks are <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_Valletta" title="Bank of Valletta">Bank of Valletta</a> and <a href="/wiki/HSBC_Bank_Malta" title="HSBC Bank Malta">HSBC Bank Malta</a>, both of which can trace their origins back to the 19th century. As of recently, <a href="/wiki/Digital_banking" title="Digital banking">digital banks</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Revolut" title="Revolut">Revolut</a> have also increased in popularity.<sup id="cite_ref-mtdigital_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mtdigital-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Malta" title="Central Bank of Malta">Central Bank of Malta</a> (Bank Ċentrali ta' Malta) has two key areas of responsibility: the formulation and implementation of monetary policy and the promotion of a sound and efficient financial system. It was established by the Central Bank of Malta Act on 17 April 1968. The Maltese government entered <a href="/wiki/ERM_II" class="mw-redirect" title="ERM II">ERM II</a> on 4 May 2005, and adopted the euro as the country's currency on 1 January 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>FinanceMalta is the quasi-governmental organisation tasked with marketing and educating business leaders coming to Malta and runs seminars and events around the world highlighting the emerging strength of Malta as a jurisdiction for banking and finance and insurance.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Malta" title="Transport in Malta">Transport in Malta</a> and <a href="/wiki/Buses_in_Malta" title="Buses in Malta">Buses in Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_of_Malta_2.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Map_of_Malta_2.png/220px-Map_of_Malta_2.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="248" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Map_of_Malta_2.png/330px-Map_of_Malta_2.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Map_of_Malta_2.png/440px-Map_of_Malta_2.png 2x" data-file-width="987" data-file-height="1114" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_of_Malta_2.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Principal internal transportation</div></div></div>
<p>Being a former British Colony, traffic in Malta <a href="/wiki/Driving_on_the_left_or_right" class="mw-redirect" title="Driving on the left or right">drives on the left</a>. Car ownership in Malta is exceedingly high, considering the very small size of the islands; it is the fourth-highest in the European Union. The number of registered cars in 1990 amounted to 182,254, giving an automobile density of <span style="display:none" data-sort-value="7002576753164556962♠"></span>577/km<sup>2</sup> (1,494/sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Malta has 2,254 kilometres (1,401 miles) of road, 1,972&#160;km (1,225&#160;mi) (87.5 per cent) of which are paved and 282&#160;km (175&#160;mi) are unpaved (as of December 2003).<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
The main roads of Malta from the southernmost point to the northernmost point are these: Triq Birżebbuġa in <a href="/wiki/Bir%C5%BCebbu%C4%A1a" title="Birżebbuġa">Birżebbuġa</a>, <a href="/wiki/G%C4%A7ar_Dalam" title="Għar Dalam">Għar Dalam</a> Road and <a href="/wiki/Tal-Barrani" title="Tal-Barrani">Tal-Barrani</a> Road in <a href="/wiki/%C5%BBejtun" title="Żejtun">Żejtun</a>, Santa Luċija Avenue in <a href="/wiki/Paola,_Malta" title="Paola, Malta">Paola</a>, Aldo Moro Street (Trunk Road), 13 December Street and <a href="/wiki/%C4%A6amrun" class="mw-redirect" title="Ħamrun">Ħamrun</a>-Marsa Bypass in <a href="/wiki/Marsa,_Malta" title="Marsa, Malta">Marsa</a>, Regional Road in <a href="/wiki/Santa_Venera" title="Santa Venera">Santa Venera</a>/<a href="/wiki/Msida" title="Msida">Msida</a>/<a href="/wiki/G%C5%BCira" title="Gżira">Gżira</a>/<a href="/wiki/San_%C4%A0wann" title="San Ġwann">San Ġwann</a>, St Andrew's Road in <a href="/wiki/Swieqi" title="Swieqi">Swieqi</a>/<a href="/wiki/Pembroke,_Malta" title="Pembroke, Malta">Pembroke, Malta</a>, Coast Road in <a href="/wiki/Ba%C4%A7ar_i%C4%8B-%C4%8Aag%C4%A7aq" title="Baħar iċ-Ċagħaq">Baħar iċ-Ċagħaq</a>, Salina Road, Kennedy Drive, St. Paul's Bypass and <a href="/wiki/Xemxija" title="Xemxija">Xemxija</a> Hill in <a href="/wiki/San_Pawl_il-Ba%C4%A7ar" class="mw-redirect" title="San Pawl il-Baħar">San Pawl il-Baħar</a>, Mistra Hill, Wettinger Street (Mellieħa Bypass) and Marfa Road in <a href="/wiki/Mellie%C4%A7a" title="Mellieħa">Mellieħa</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_Buses_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="119" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Malta_Buses_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4378" data-file-height="2369" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta_Buses_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Maltese <i><a href="/wiki/Otokar" title="Otokar">Otokar</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/King_Long" title="King Long">King Long</a></i> buses</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Malta_bus" class="mw-redirect" title="Malta bus">Buses</a> (<i>xarabank</i> or <i>karozza tal-linja</i>) are the primary method of public transport, established in 1905. Malta's vintage buses operated in the Maltese islands up to 2011 and became popular tourist attractions in their own right.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> To this day they are depicted on many Maltese advertisements to promote tourism as well as on gifts and merchandise for tourists.
</p><p>The bus service underwent extensive reform in July 2011. The management structure changed from having self-employed drivers driving their own vehicles to a service being offered by a single company through a public tender (in Gozo, being considered a small network, the service was given through direct order).<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> The public tender was won by <a href="/wiki/Arriva_Malta" class="mw-redirect" title="Arriva Malta">Arriva Malta</a>, a member of the <a href="/wiki/Arriva" title="Arriva">Arriva</a> group, which introduced a fleet of brand new buses, built by <a href="/wiki/King_Long" title="King Long">King Long</a> especially for service by Arriva Malta and including a smaller fleet of <a href="/wiki/Articulated_bus" title="Articulated bus">articulated buses</a> brought in from <a href="/wiki/Arriva_London" title="Arriva London">Arriva London</a>. It also operated two smaller buses for an intra-Valletta route only and 61 nine-metre buses, which were used to ease congestion on high-density routes. Overall Arriva Malta operated 264 buses. On 1 January 2014 Arriva ceased operations in Malta due to financial difficulties, having been nationalised as <i>Malta Public Transport</i> by the Maltese government, with a new bus operator planned to take over their operations.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> The government chose Autobuses Urbanos de León (<a href="/wiki/ALSA_(bus_company)" title="ALSA (bus company)">ALSA</a> subsidiary) as its preferred bus operator for the country in October 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> The company took over the bus service on 8 January 2015, while retaining the name <i>Malta Public Transport</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> It introduced the pre-pay 'tallinja card'. With lower fares than the walk-on rate, it can be topped up online. The card was initially not well received, as reported by several local news sites.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> During the first week of August 2015, another 40 buses of the Turkish make <i><a href="/wiki/Otokar" title="Otokar">Otokar</a></i> arrived and were put into service.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From October 2022 the bus system will be free of charge for residents of Malta.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1883 to 1931 Malta had a <a href="/wiki/Malta_Railway" title="Malta Railway">railway</a> line that connected Valletta to the army barracks at <a href="/wiki/Mtarfa" title="Mtarfa">Mtarfa</a> via <a href="/wiki/Mdina" title="Mdina">Mdina</a> and a number of towns and villages. The railway fell into disuse and eventually closed altogether, following the introduction of electric trams and buses.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> At the height of the bombing of Malta during the Second World War, <a href="/wiki/Benito_Mussolini" title="Benito Mussolini">Mussolini</a> announced that his forces had destroyed the railway system, but by the time war broke out, the railway had been mothballed for more than nine years.
</p><p>As of 2021, an underground <a href="/wiki/Malta_Metro" title="Malta Metro">Malta Metro</a> is being planned, with a projected total cost of €6.2 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Valletta-Harbour.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Valletta-Harbour.jpg/220px-Valletta-Harbour.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="97" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Valletta-Harbour.jpg/330px-Valletta-Harbour.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Valletta-Harbour.jpg/440px-Valletta-Harbour.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="527" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Valletta-Harbour.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Grand_Harbour" title="Grand Harbour">Grand Harbour</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:047.Freeport.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/047.Freeport.jpg/220px-047.Freeport.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/047.Freeport.jpg/330px-047.Freeport.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/047.Freeport.jpg/440px-047.Freeport.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="530" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:047.Freeport.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Malta_Freeport" title="Malta Freeport">Malta Freeport</a>, one of the largest European ports</div></div></div>
<p>Malta has three large natural harbours on its main island:
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Grand_Harbour" title="Grand Harbour">Grand Harbour</a> (or Port il-Kbir), located at the eastern side of the capital city of Valletta, has been a harbour since <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman</a> times. It has several extensive <a href="/wiki/Dock_(maritime)" class="mw-redirect" title="Dock (maritime)">docks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wharf" title="Wharf">wharves</a>, as well as a cruise liner terminal. A terminal at the Grand Harbour serves ferries that connect Malta to <a href="/wiki/Pozzallo" title="Pozzallo">Pozzallo</a> &amp; <a href="/wiki/Catania" title="Catania">Catania</a> in Sicily.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marsamxett_Harbour" title="Marsamxett Harbour">Marsamxett Harbour</a>, located on the western side of Valletta, accommodates a number of yacht marinas.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marsaxlokk_Harbour" class="mw-redirect" title="Marsaxlokk Harbour">Marsaxlokk Harbour</a> (Malta Freeport), at <a href="/wiki/Bir%C5%BCebbu%C4%A1a" title="Birżebbuġa">Birżebbuġa</a> on the south-eastern side of Malta, is the islands' main cargo terminal. Malta Freeport is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_world%27s_busiest_container_ports" class="mw-redirect" title="List of world&#39;s busiest container ports">11th busiest container ports in continent of Europe and 46th in the World</a> with a trade volume of 2.3&#160;million <a href="/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit" title="Twenty-foot equivalent unit">TEU's</a> in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>There are also two man-made harbours that serve a passenger and car ferry service that connects <a href="/wiki/%C4%8Airkewwa" title="Ċirkewwa">Ċirkewwa</a> Harbour on Malta and <a href="/wiki/M%C4%A1arr_(Gozo)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mġarr (Gozo)">Mġarr Harbour</a> on <a href="/wiki/Gozo" title="Gozo">Gozo</a>. The ferry makes numerous runs each day.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Malta_International_Airport" title="Malta International Airport">Malta International Airport</a> (Ajruport Internazzjonali ta' Malta) is the only airport serving the Maltese islands. It is built on the land formerly occupied by the <a href="/wiki/RAF_Luqa" title="RAF Luqa">RAF Luqa</a> air base. A heliport is also located there, but the scheduled service to Gozo ceased in 2006. The heliport in Gozo is at <a href="/wiki/Xewkija" title="Xewkija">Xewkija</a>.
</p><p>Two further airfields at <a href="/wiki/Ta%27_Qali" title="Ta&#39; Qali">Ta' Qali</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C4%A6al_Far" title="Ħal Far">Ħal Far</a> operated during the Second World War and into the 1960s but are now closed. Today, <a href="/wiki/Ta%27_Qali" title="Ta&#39; Qali">Ta' Qali</a> houses a national park, <a href="/wiki/Ta%27_Qali_Stadium" class="mw-redirect" title="Ta&#39; Qali Stadium">stadium</a>, the Crafts Village visitor attraction and the <a href="/wiki/Malta_Aviation_Museum" title="Malta Aviation Museum">Malta Aviation Museum</a>. This museum preserves several aircraft, including <a href="/wiki/Hawker_Hurricane" title="Hawker Hurricane">Hurricane</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spitfire" class="mw-redirect" title="Spitfire">Spitfire</a> fighters that defended the island in the Second World War.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_(14806272801).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg/220px-9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg/330px-9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg/440px-9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_%2814806272801%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="683" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:9H-AEP_A320_Air_Malta_(14806272801).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An Air Malta <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A320" class="mw-redirect" title="Airbus A320">Airbus A320</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The national airline is <a href="/wiki/Air_Malta" title="Air Malta">Air Malta</a>, which is based at Malta International Airport and operates services to 36 destinations in Europe and North Africa. The owners of Air Malta are the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Malta" title="Government of Malta">Government of Malta</a> (98 percent) and private investors (2 percent). Air Malta employs 1,547 staff along with having a 25 percent share in <a href="/wiki/Medavia" title="Medavia">Medavia</a>.
</p><p>Air Malta has concluded over 191 interline ticketing agreements with other <a href="/wiki/International_Air_Transport_Association" title="International Air Transport Association">IATA</a> airlines. It also has a codeshare agreement with <a href="/wiki/Qantas" title="Qantas">Qantas</a> covering three routes. In September 2007, Air Malta made two agreements with Abu Dhabi-based Etihad Airways by which Air Malta wet-leased two Airbus aircraft to <a href="/wiki/Etihad_Airways" title="Etihad Airways">Etihad Airways</a> for the winter period starting 1 September 2007, and provided operational support on another Airbus A320 aircraft which it leased to Etihad Airways.
</p><p>In June 2019, <a href="/wiki/Ryanair" title="Ryanair">Ryanair</a> has invested into a fully-fledged airline subsidiary, called <a href="/wiki/Malta_Air" title="Malta Air">Malta Air</a>, operating a low-cost model. The Government of Malta holds one share in the airline whereby it holds rights to the brand name.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span></h3>
<p>The mobile penetration rate in Malta exceeded 100% by the end of 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Malta uses the <a href="/wiki/GSM" title="GSM">GSM</a>900, UMTS(3G) and LTE(4G) mobile phone systems, which are compatible with the rest of the European countries, Australia and New Zealand.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Telephone and cellular subscriber numbers have eight digits. There are no <a href="/wiki/Area_code" class="mw-redirect" title="Area code">area codes</a> in Malta, but after inception, the original first two numbers, and currently<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (December 2019)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> the 3rd and 4th digit, were assigned according to the locality. <a href="/wiki/Fixed_line" class="mw-redirect" title="Fixed line">Fixed line</a> telephone numbers have the prefix 21 and 27, although businesses may have numbers starting 22 or 23. An example would be 2*80**** if from <a href="/wiki/%C5%BBabbar" title="Żabbar">Żabbar</a>, and 2*23**** if from <a href="/wiki/Marsa,_Malta" title="Marsa, Malta">Marsa</a>. Gozitan landline numbers generally are assigned 2*56****. Mobile telephone numbers have the prefix 77, 79, 98 or 99. Malta's international calling code is +356.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The number of pay-TV subscribers fell as customers switched to <a href="/wiki/IPTV" class="mw-redirect" title="IPTV">Internet Protocol television</a> (IPTV): the number of IPTV subscribers doubled in the six months to June 2012.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In early 2012, the government called for a national Fibre to the Home (FttH) network to be built, with a minimum broadband service being upgraded from 4Mbit/s to 100Mbit/s.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Currency">Currency</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Maltese_euro_coins" title="Maltese euro coins">Maltese euro coins</a> and <a href="/wiki/Euro_gold_and_silver_commemorative_coins_(Malta)" title="Euro gold and silver commemorative coins (Malta)">Euro gold and silver commemorative coins (Malta)</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Maltese_euro_coins" title="Maltese euro coins">Maltese euro coins</a> feature the <a href="/wiki/Maltese_cross" title="Maltese cross">Maltese cross</a> on €2 and €1 coins, the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Malta" title="Coat of arms of Malta">coat of arms of Malta</a> on the €0.50, €0.20 and €0.10 coins, and the <a href="/wiki/Mnajdra" title="Mnajdra">Mnajdra</a> Temples on the €0.05, €0.02 and €0.01 coins.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Malta has produced collectors' coins with face value ranging from 10 to 50 euros. These coins continue an existing national practice of minting of silver and gold commemorative coins. Unlike normal issues, these coins are not accepted in all the eurozone. For instance, a €10 Maltese commemorative coin cannot be used in any other country.
</p><p>From its introduction in 1972 until the introduction of the Euro in 2008, the currency was the <a href="/wiki/Maltese_lira" title="Maltese lira">Maltese lira</a>, which had replaced the Maltese pound. The pound replaced the <a href="/wiki/Maltese_scudo" title="Maltese scudo">Maltese scudo</a> in 1825.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg/220px-Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg/330px-Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg/440px-Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="1667" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mellieha_Bay_beach_Malta_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mellieħa Bay beach</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Malta" title="Tourism in Malta">Tourism in Malta</a></div>
<p>Malta is a popular tourist destination, with 1.6&#160;million tourists per year.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> Three times more tourists visit than there are residents. Tourism infrastructure has increased dramatically over the years and a number of hotels are present on the island, although overdevelopment and the destruction of traditional housing is of growing concern. An increasing number of Maltese now travel abroad on holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, Malta had a record year in tourism, recording over 2.1 million tourists in one single year.<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In recent years, Malta has advertised itself as a <a href="/wiki/Medical_tourism" title="Medical tourism">medical tourism</a> destination,<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> and a number of <a href="/wiki/Health_tourism_provider" class="mw-redirect" title="Health tourism provider">health tourism providers</a> are developing the industry. However, no Maltese hospital has undergone independent <a href="/wiki/International_healthcare_accreditation" title="International healthcare accreditation">international healthcare accreditation</a>. Malta is popular with British medical tourists,<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> pointing Maltese hospitals towards seeking UK-sourced accreditation, such as with the <a href="/wiki/Trent_Accreditation_Scheme" title="Trent Accreditation Scheme">Trent Accreditation Scheme</a>.
</p><p>Tourism in Malta contributes to around 11.6 percent of the country's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">gross domestic product</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Science_and_technology">Science and technology</span></h3>
<p>Malta signed a co-operation agreement with the <a href="/wiki/European_Space_Agency" title="European Space Agency">European Space Agency</a> (ESA) for more-intensive co-operation in ESA projects.<sup id="cite_ref-Malta_signs_Cooperation_Agreement_with_ESA_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Malta_signs_Cooperation_Agreement_with_ESA-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup>
The Malta Council for Science and Technology (MCST) is the civil body responsible for the development of science and technology on an educational and social level. Most science students in Malta graduate from the University of Malta and are represented by S-Cubed (Science Student's Society), UESA (University Engineering Students Association) and ICTSA (University of Malta ICT Students' Association).<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Malta was ranked 21st in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2022, up from 27th in 2019, 2020 and 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Malta" title="Demographics of Malta">Demographics of Malta</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:402px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta,_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_(23).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg/400px-Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="400" height="200" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg/600px-Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg/800px-Malta%2C_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_%2823%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5000" data-file-height="2500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Malta,_2010_-_panoramio_-_Bengt_Nyman_(23).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Valletta, Malta's capital</div></div></div>
<p>A population and housing census is conducted every ten years in Malta. The November 2005 census counted an estimated 96 percent of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> A preliminary report was issued in April 2006 and the results were weighted to estimate for 100 percent of the population.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Maltese_people" title="Maltese people">Maltese</a> natives make up the majority of the island. However, there are minorities, the largest of which are <a href="/wiki/British_people" title="British people">Britons</a>, many of whom are retirees.
The population of Malta as of July&#160;2011<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Malta&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> was estimated at 408,000.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_35-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> As of 2005<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Malta&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 17 percent were aged 14 and under, 68 percent were within the 15–64 age bracket whilst the remaining 13 percent were 65 years and over. Malta's population density of 1,282 per square km (3,322/sq&#160;mi) is by far the highest in the EU and one of the highest in the world. By comparison, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_population_density" class="mw-redirect" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by population density">average population density</a> for the World (land only, excluding <a href="/wiki/Antarctica" title="Antarctica">Antarctica</a>) was 54/km<sup>2</sup> (140/sq&#160;mi) as of July 2014.
</p><p>The only census year showing a fall in population was that of 1967, with a 1.7 per cent total decrease, attributable to a substantial number of Maltese residents who emigrated.<sup id="cite_ref-Census2005_241-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2005-241">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> The Maltese-resident population for 2004 was estimated to make up 97.0 per cent of the total resident population.<sup id="cite_ref-Demo2004_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Demo2004-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>All censuses since 1842 have shown a slight excess of females over males. The 1901 and 1911 censuses came closest to recording a balance. The highest female-to-male ratio was reached in 1957 (1088:1000) but since then the ratio has dropped continuously. The 2005 census showed a 1013:1000 female-to-male ratio.
Population growth has slowed down, from +9.5 per cent between the 1985 and 1995 censuses, to +6.9 per cent between the 1995 and 2005 censuses (a yearly average of +0.7 per cent). The birth rate stood at 3860 (a decrease of 21.8 per cent from the 1995 census) and the death rate stood at 3025. Thus, there was a natural population increase of 835 (compared to +888 for 2004, of which over a hundred were foreign residents).<sup id="cite_ref-NSO_20060711_243-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NSO_20060711-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The population's age composition is similar to the age structure prevalent in the EU. Since 1967 there was observed a trend indicating an ageing population, and is expected to continue in the foreseeable future. Malta's <a href="/wiki/Dependency_ratio" title="Dependency ratio">old-age-dependency-ratio</a> rose from 17.2 percent in 1995 to 19.8 percent in 2005, reasonably lower than the EU's 24.9 percent average; 31.5 percent of the Maltese population is aged under 25 (compared to the EU's 29.1 percent); but the 50–64 age group constitutes 20.3 percent of the population, significantly higher than the EU's 17.9 percent. Malta's old-age-dependency-ratio is expected to continue rising steadily in the coming years.
</p><p>Maltese legislation recognises both civil and canonical (ecclesiastical) marriages. Annulments by the ecclesiastical and civil courts are unrelated and are not necessarily mutually endorsed. Malta voted in favour of divorce legislation in <a href="/wiki/2011_Maltese_divorce_referendum" title="2011 Maltese divorce referendum">a referendum held on 28 May 2011</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Abortion_in_Malta" title="Abortion in Malta">Abortion in Malta</a> is illegal. It's the only European Union member state with a total ban on the procedure. There are no exceptions, including in cases of rape or incest.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> On 21 November 2022, the government led by the Labour Party proposed a bill that "introduces a new clause into the country’s criminal code allowing for the termination of a pregnancy if the mother’s life is at risk or if her health is in serious jeopardy".<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A person must be 16 to marry.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> The number of brides aged under 25 decreased from 1471 in 1997 to 766 in 2005; while the number of grooms under 25 decreased from 823 to 311. There is a constant trend that females are more likely than males to marry young. In 2005 there were 51 brides aged between 16 and 19, compared to 8 grooms.<sup id="cite_ref-NSO_20060711_243-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NSO_20060711-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2021, the population of the Maltese Islands stood at 516,100.<sup id="cite_ref-NSO_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NSO-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (TFR) as of 2016<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Malta&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> was estimated at 1.45 children born/woman, which is below the replacement rate of 2.1.<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_248-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-248">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, 25.8 per cent of births were to unmarried women.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> in 2018 was estimated at 83.<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Il-Kantilena.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Il-Kantilena.png/220px-Il-Kantilena.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="324" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Il-Kantilena.png/330px-Il-Kantilena.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Il-Kantilena.png/440px-Il-Kantilena.png 2x" data-file-width="680" data-file-height="1000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Il-Kantilena.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i><a href="/wiki/Il-Kantilena" title="Il-Kantilena">Il-Kantilena</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Pietru_Caxaro" title="Pietru Caxaro">Pietru Caxaro</a>, the oldest text in <a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese language</a>, 15th century</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Malta" title="Languages of Malta">Languages of Malta</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="#Education">§&#160;Education</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese language</a> (<a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese</a>: <i lang="mt">Malti</i>) is one of the two constitutional <a href="/wiki/Languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Languages">languages</a> of Malta, having become official, however, only in 1934, and being considered as the national language. Previously, <a href="/wiki/Sicilian_language" title="Sicilian language">Sicilian</a> was the official and cultural language of Malta from the 12th century, and the <a href="/wiki/Tuscan_dialect" title="Tuscan dialect">Tuscan dialect</a> of <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a> from the 16th century. Alongside Maltese, English is also an official language of the country and hence the laws of the land are enacted both in Maltese and English. However, article 74 of the Constitution states that "... if there is any conflict between the Maltese and the English texts of any law, the Maltese text shall prevail."<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_34-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Maltese is a <a href="/wiki/Semitic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Semitic language">Semitic language</a> descended from the now extinct Sicilian-Arabic (<a href="/wiki/Siculo-Arabic" title="Siculo-Arabic">Siculo-Arabic</a>) dialect (from <a href="/wiki/Southern_Italy" title="Southern Italy">southern Italy</a>) that developed during the <a href="/wiki/Emirate_of_Sicily" title="Emirate of Sicily">Emirate of Sicily</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-MED_251-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MED-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Maltese_alphabet" title="Maltese alphabet">Maltese alphabet</a> consists of 30 letters based on the <a href="/wiki/Latin_alphabet" title="Latin alphabet">Latin alphabet</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/Diacritic" title="Diacritic">diacritically</a> altered letters <i><a href="/wiki/%C5%BB" title="Ż">ż</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/%C4%8A" title="Ċ">ċ</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/%C4%A0" title="Ġ">ġ</a></i>, as well as the letters <i><a href="/wiki/G%C4%A7" class="mw-redirect" title="Għ">għ</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/%C4%A6" class="mw-redirect" title="Ħ">ħ</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Ie_(digraph)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ie (digraph)">ie</a></i>.
</p><p>Maltese is the only <a href="/wiki/Semitic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Semitic language">Semitic language</a> with official status in the European Union. Maltese has a Semitic base with substantial borrowing from <a href="/wiki/Sicilian_language" title="Sicilian language">Sicilian</a>, Italian, a little French, and more recently and increasingly, English.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> The hybrid character of Maltese was established by a long period of Maltese-Sicilian urban bilingualism gradually transforming rural speech and which ended in the early 19th century with Maltese emerging as the vernacular of the entire native population. The language includes different dialects that can vary greatly from one town to another or from one island to another.
</p><p>In 2012, the <a href="/wiki/Eurobarometer" title="Eurobarometer">Eurobarometer</a> states that 97 percent of the Maltese population consider Maltese as mother tongue. Also, 89 percent of the population speak English, 66 percent speak Italian, and 17 percent speak French.<sup id="cite_ref-europa.eu_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-europa.eu-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> This widespread knowledge of <a href="/wiki/Second_language" title="Second language">second languages</a> makes Malta one of the most multilingual countries in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>. A study collecting public opinion on what language was "preferred" discovered that 86 percent of the population express a preference for Maltese, 12 percent for English, and 2 percent for Italian.<sup id="cite_ref-LinguisticView_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LinguisticView-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> Still, Italian television channels from Italy-based broadcasters, such as <a href="/wiki/Mediaset" title="Mediaset">Mediaset</a> and <a href="/wiki/RAI" title="RAI">RAI</a>, reach Malta and remain popular.<sup id="cite_ref-LinguisticView_253-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LinguisticView-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Maltese_Sign_Language" title="Maltese Sign Language">Maltese Sign Language</a> is used by signers in Malta.<sup id="cite_ref-paggio_256-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-paggio-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Malta" title="Religion in Malta">Religion in Malta</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Malta" title="History of the Jews in Malta">History of the Jews in Malta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Malta" title="Christianity in Malta">Christianity in Malta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Malta" title="Islam in Malta">Islam in Malta</a></div>
